I haven't really noticed feeling down or, you know, wanting to cry or anything like that. It's mostly just what I mentioned... my brain just feels stuck.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I don't feel nervous or panicked or anything like that. I'm not sitting at my desk worrying about things. It's really just that blank, stuck feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel physically exhausted or sleepy during the day or anything like that. I get up and go to work just fine, and I have normal energy, I guess... it's just that mental block once I'm actually sitting at my desk.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Like... my 3D printer for miniatures has just been sitting with the same half-finished figure on it for months now. I haven't even touched it. And other things I usually like, like yoga or going for a walk in the woods... it just feels like another task to get through instead of something I actually want to do.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this stuck, mentally blocked feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since I was moved into the prototype-testing role at work. That was... about a year and a month ago, I think. Yeah, right around then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness or guilt?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I keep replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab over and over in my head. And I end up telling myself that I'm not just bad at this job, but that I'm actually a bad person. It just feels like I deserve whatever consequences come from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It happens mostly on my commute. I'll just catch myself thinking that it would be easier if I just didn't arrive anywhere. And sometimes... the idea of just steering off the road pops into my head. It actually scares me enough that I end up gripping the wheel really tight and having to pull over for a minute.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the stuck feeling, lack of interest in activities, and thoughts of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, it all pretty much started when I got moved into that prototype-testing role at work. So, yeah, it's been about a year and a month now of dealing with all of this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt better and your mental block, lack of interest, and thoughts of worthlessness were significantly reduced?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been a constant thing since it started. I haven't had any real break from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where these feelings of being stuck, worthless, and having thoughts of death were so severe that you couldn't get out of bed or perform daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I usually manage to force myself out of bed eventually, but as for performing daily tasks... yeah, it's definitely impacting that. I mean, at work, I actually missed a key project deadline recently. I just couldn't pull it together. Now I have to ask my coworkers to double-check my work, or even take over parts of the reports that I used to finish completely on my own. \n\nAnd outside of the office, it's pretty much the same. I've mostly stopped seeing people, and I just... I dropped the daily routines that used to keep me steady. It actually scares me enough that I end up gripping the wheel really tight and having to pull over for a minute.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the stuck feeling, lack of interest in activities, and thoughts of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, it all pretty much started when I got moved into that prototype-testing role at work. So, yeah, it's been about a year and a month now of dealing with all of this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt better and your mental block, lack of interest, and thoughts of worthlessness were significantly reduced?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been a constant thing since it started. I haven't had any real break from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where these feelings of being stuck, worthless, and having thoughts of death were so severe that you couldn't get out of bed or perform daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Well, I usually manage to force myself out of bed eventually, but as for performing daily tasks... yeah, it's definitely impacting that. I mean, at work, I actually missed a key project deadline recently. I just couldn't pull it together. Now I have to ask my coworkers to double-check my work, or even take over parts of the reports that I used to finish completely on my own. \n\nAnd outside of the office, it's pretty much the same. I've mostly stopped seeing people, and I just... I dropped the daily routines that used to keep me steady. So, yes, it's definitely making it hard to function normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things that you used to find pleasure in, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's definitely changed. I have a 3D printer for miniatures, and it's just been sitting there with the same half-finished figure on it for months now. I just haven't touched it. And even physical stuff I used to like, such as yoga or going for a walk in the woods... they just feel like another task I have to get through, instead of something I actually want to do. I don't really get any enjoyment out of them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you didn't feel this way, before you started feeling stuck and worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I didn't always feel like this. It really started... well, this has all been going on since I was moved into the prototype-testing role at work. That was about a year and a month ago. Before that change, things were normal and I didn't feel stuck like this at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before the change in your job role about a year and a month ago?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, not like this. Before I got moved to the prototype testing a year and a month ago, everything was fine. I didn't have these feelings before then.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being stuck and worthless usually lasted for most of the day, rather than just a little part of it?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't exactly kept track of it like that. I mean, I definitely have those feelings, but... I haven't really noticed if it's lasting for most of the day or just parts of it. I just haven't paid that much attention to the timing.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of being stuck and worthless been present for at least two weeks at any point since they started?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about that either.
